# English 1 Unit and Lesson Map

**Status:** Approved Savvas-aligned course architecture (2026-08-03)  
**Primary curriculum:** Savvas Realize: myPerspectives English Language Arts, Grade 9

## Course Structure Summary

| Unit | Unit Title | Unit Purpose | Lesson Count | Unit Pretest | Unit Assessment |
|---|---|---|---:|---|---|
| Unit 1 | Literary and Informational Foundations | Already-built foundational unit; retained unchanged. | 5 | Required | Required |
| Unit 2 | Inside the Nightmare | Analyze Gothic literature and informational texts, then craft and present a fear narrative. | 9 | Required | Required |
| Unit 3 | Outsiders and Outcasts | Analyze outsider identity and produce a source-supported argument and multimedia presentation. | 10 | Required | Required |
| Unit 4 | Crazy, Stupid Love | Analyze tragedy, adaptation, poetry, argument, and media through Romeo and Juliet and related texts. | 12 | Required | Required |
| Unit 5 | Journeys of Transformation | Analyze epic and journey traditions, conduct research, and publish an expository synthesis. | 11 | Required | Required |
| Unit 6 | World’s End | Analyze speculative and apocalyptic texts, write fiction and exposition, and create a visual presentation. | 9 | Required | Required |

## Total Course Counts

| Item | Count |
|---|---:|
| Units | 6 |
| Lessons | 56 |
| Unit Pretests | 6 |
| Notebook Evidence Submissions | 56 |
| Checkpoints | 56 |
| Lesson or Cluster Quizzes | 12 in Units 2–6, plus retained Unit 1 quizzes |
| Unit Assessments | 6 |
| Projects/Labs/Performance Tasks | 6 cumulative unit synthesis products |

## Governing Architecture Rules

- Unit 1 contains five already-built foundational lessons and is locked.
- Do not create blank replacement lessons or revise, rebuild, remap, or replace Unit 1 without new academic approval.
- This file records Unit 1 only at course-summary level because no Unit 1 production work is authorized.
- Units 2–6 contain 51 approved detailed lesson records.
- Every new lesson uses the approved seven-page English pattern and ends with Checkpoint and Help & TOR Support.
- Every unit ends with cumulative synthesis and the Unit Assessment.
- Moodle is the official evidence record; protected teacher answers remain inaccessible to students.
- COURSE_OVERVIEW.md, COURSE_CROSSWALK.md, and this file jointly govern production.

# Unit 1 — Literary and Informational Foundations

## Unit Status

Unit 1 is already built and approved. It contains five foundational lessons. Its existing pretest, lesson content, Notebook Evidence, Checkpoints, quizzes, synthesis, and Unit Assessment remain unchanged.

## Production Scope

No new Unit 1 lesson specifications are created in this document. The Moodle Course Builder must not add blank lessons, reconstruct lesson details, or alter the existing Unit 1 implementation without a later approved academic revision.

---

# Unit 2 — Inside the Nightmare

## Unit Purpose

Analyze Gothic literature and informational texts, then craft and present a fear narrative.

## Relationship to Prior and Future Units

This unit builds on Unit 1 foundations and earlier course learning and prepares students for Outsiders and Outcasts.

## Unit Learning Objectives

By the end of this unit, students will be able to:

1. Analyze the unit’s literary, informational, visual, audio, or multimedia sources.
2. Apply writing, language, research, and communication standards in authentic products.
3. Integrate unit learning in a synthesis and Unit Assessment.

## Unit Moodle Sequence

1. Unit 2 Overview
2. Unit 2 Pretest
3. Lesson 2.1 — Entering the Gothic: Fear, Setting, and Essential Questions
4. Lesson 2.2 — Diction, Syntax, and Mood in “The Fall of the House of Usher”
5. Lesson 2.3 — Literary Elements, Theme, and Sentence Control in “Usher”
6. Lesson 2.4 — Perspective and Cultural Comparison in “House Taken Over”
7. Lesson 2.5 — Visual Text Structure and Gothic Conventions
8. Lesson 2.6 — Narrative Point of View and Suspense in “Where Is Here?”
9. Lesson 2.7 — Fear Through Literary and Scientific Lenses
10. Lesson 2.8 — Figurative Language, Sound, and Mood in Gothic Poetry
11. Lesson 2.9 — Crafting and Presenting a Fear Narrative
12. Unit Completion and Next-Step Instructions

# Unit 2, Lesson 1 — Entering the Gothic: Fear, Setting, and Essential Questions

## Lesson Identification

| Field | Requirement |
|---|---|
| MLA Lesson ID | ENG1-U2-L1 |
| Savvas Source | Introduction: Unit 1; Mentor Text: My Introduction to Gothic Literature |
| Estimated Scope | Seven-page approved English pattern A, P1-P7 |
| Prerequisite Knowledge | Prior lessons in Unit 2 and prerequisite evidence from the Unit Pretest |
| Leads To | The next approved lesson and Unit 2 synthesis |

## Lesson Purpose

Establish genre expectations, paraphrase, and academic vocabulary; R.10 introduced, V.01 introduced

## Learning Objectives

By the end of the lesson, students will be able to:

1. Explain the central reading, writing, language, research, or communication concept.
2. Apply the concept to the exact assigned Savvas text, source, or performance task.
3. Produce and justify the required evidence: Vocabulary, paraphrase, genre notes; explain how one Gothic convention shapes expectations.

## Standards

| Standard | Instructional Role | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| MLA.ENG1.R.10, MLA.ENG1.V.01, MLA.ENG1.L.01 | As approved in the source record and COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Notebook Evidence; Checkpoint; |

# Unit 3 — Outsiders and Outcasts

## Unit Purpose

Analyze outsider identity and produce a source-supported argument and multimedia presentation.

## Relationship to Prior and Future Units

This unit builds on Unit 1 foundations and earlier course learning and prepares students for Crazy, Stupid Love.

## Unit Learning Objectives

By the end of this unit, students will be able to:

1. Analyze the unit’s literary, informational, visual, audio, or multimedia sources.
2. Apply writing, language, research, and communication standards in authentic products.
3. Integrate unit learning in a synthesis and Unit Assessment.

# Unit 4 — Crazy, Stupid Love

## Unit Purpose

Analyze tragedy, adaptation, poetry, argument, and media through Romeo and Juliet and related texts.

## Relationship to Prior and Future Units

This unit builds on Unit 1 foundations and earlier course learning and prepares students for Journeys of Transformation.

## Unit Learning Objectives

By the end of this unit, students will be able to:

1. Analyze the unit’s literary, informational, visual, audio, or multimedia sources.
2. Apply writing, language, research, and communication standards in authentic products.
3. Integrate unit learning in a synthesis and Unit Assessment.

# Unit 5 — Journeys of Transformation

## Unit Purpose

Analyze epic and journey traditions, conduct research, and publish an expository synthesis.

## Relationship to Prior and Future Units

This unit builds on Unit 1 foundations and earlier course learning and prepares students for World’s End.

## Unit Learning Objectives

By the end of this unit, students will be able to:

1. Analyze the unit’s literary, informational, visual, audio, or multimedia sources.
2. Apply writing, language, research, and communication standards in authentic products.
3. Integrate unit learning in a synthesis and Unit Assessment.

# Unit 6 — World’s End

## Unit Purpose

Analyze speculative and apocalyptic texts, write fiction and exposition, and create a visual presentation.

## Relationship to Prior and Future Units

This unit builds on Unit 1 foundations and earlier course learning and prepares students for later high-school English, college and career communication, and assessment readiness.

## Unit Learning Objectives

By the end of this unit, students will be able to:

1. Analyze the unit’s literary, informational, visual, audio, or multimedia sources.
2. Apply writing, language, research, and communication standards in authentic products.
3. Integrate unit learning in a synthesis and Unit Assessment.
